W^{1,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)
and then the interpolation and (
5.225
)
4
we obtain

(5.230)

\|\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{\beta}(0,T;L^{3\beta}(\Omega))}+\|\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{p_{\beta}}(Q_{T})}\leq c,\text{ with }p_{\beta}=\frac{8}{3}+\beta.

By (
5.121
) we get

(5.231)

\begin{split}\underline{a}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\leq\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\leq\overline{a}\mathfrak{r},\\
\underline{b}\Sigma_{\delta}\leq\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\leq\overline{b}\Sigma_{\delta},\\
\underline{d}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\leq\xi_{\delta}\leq\overline{d}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}.\end{split}

Taking into account (
1.12
) we write

\mathbb{S}(\vartheta_{\delta},\nabla u_{\delta})=\sqrt{\mu(\vartheta_{\delta})\vartheta_{\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}}\left(\nabla u+(\nabla u)^{\top}-\frac{2}{3}\operatorname{div}u\mathbb{I}\right)+\sqrt{\eta(\vartheta_{\delta})\vartheta_{\delta}}\sqrt{\frac{\eta(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}}\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}\mathbb{I}.

Using (
5.226
)
1
and (
5.229
)
1
we get
by (
2.2
) and (
2.3
)

\|\mathbb{S}(\vartheta_{\delta},\nabla u_{\delta})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c

with
q=\frac{2p_{\beta}}{2+p_{\beta}}>\frac{4}{3}
due to (
2.18
).
Next, we use a test function of the form
\varphi=\phi\mathcal{B}\left(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{\mathfrak{c}}-\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right)
, where
\phi\in C^{1}_{c}((0,T))
,
\mathcal{B}
is the Bogovskii operator and
\mathfrak{c}\leq\gamma_{BOG}
with
\gamma_{BOG}
given by (
2.16
) to obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{P}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{\mathfrak{c}}\leq c.

The latter bound, (
5.16
) and (
5.231
) imply

(5.232)

\|\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}}(Q_{T})}+\|\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}}(Q_{T})}+\delta^{\frac{1}{\Gamma}}\left(\|\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\overline{\Gamma}}(Q_{T})}+\|\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\overline{\Gamma}}(Q_{T})}\right)\leq c,\ \overline{\gamma}=\gamma+\gamma_{BOG},\ \overline{\Gamma}=\Gamma+\gamma_{BOG}

due to (
5.16
) and (
2.10
). By virtue of (
5.230
) and (
5.232
) we infer

(5.233)

\|\mathcal{P}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c,\text{ for some }q>1.

Employing (
2.7
) we have

(5.234)

\|\mathcal{E}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c\text{ for some }q>1

due to (
5.230
) and (
5.232
).
By (
2.6
) and (
2.9
) we have

|\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})|\leq c(1+\vartheta_{\delta}^{3}+\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}|\log\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}|+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}|\log\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}|+(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})|\log\vartheta_{\delta}|+\mathfrak{r}^{\gamma^{s}_{+}}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}^{\gamma^{s}_{-}}_{\delta}).

Hence using (
5.225
)
2,3,4
and (
5.228
)

(5.235)

\|\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq c\text{ for some }q>\frac{6}{5}.

We also have

(5.236)

\|(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})\log\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{p}(Q_{T})}\leq c\delta^{-\frac{1}{\Gamma}}\text{ for }p\in(\frac{6}{5},2)

by (
5.225
)
3
and (
5.228
).
Moreover,

\begin{split}|\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})u_{\delta}|\leq c(|u_{\delta}|+|\vartheta_{\delta}|^{3}|u_{\delta}|+(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}|\log\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}|+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}|\log\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}|)|u_{\delta}|).\end{split}

Using (
5.225
)
2,4
, (
5.227
), the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)
we get

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})u_{\delta}\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c\text{ for some }q>1.\end{split}

Moreover, applying (
5.225
)
3
we have

(5.237)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})|\log\vartheta_{\delta}|(1+|u_{\delta}|)\\
&\leq(\|\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\Gamma}(\Omega))}+\|\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\Gamma}(\Omega))})\|\log\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\left(1+\|u_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))}\right)\leq c\delta^{-\frac{1}{\Gamma}}.\end{split}

By (
2.4
) it follows that

\frac{\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}|\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}|\leq c(|\nabla\log\vartheta_{\delta}|+\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}|\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}|).

Thanks to (
5.228
), (
5.229
)
1
and (
5.230
) we have the bound

\left\|\frac{\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c\text{ with }q=\frac{2p_{\beta}}{\beta+p_{\beta}}.

We observe that

\vartheta_{\delta}^{\Gamma-1}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}=\frac{2}{\Gamma}\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{1}{4}}\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\Gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\Gamma}{2}}.

Hence we estimate using (
5.225
)
4
, (
5.229
)
2
and the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{6}(\Omega)

(5.238)

\delta\|\vartheta_{\delta}^{\Gamma-1}\nabla\vartheta^{\frac{\Gamma}{2}}\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\leq\frac{2\delta}{\Gamma}\|\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega))}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\Gamma}{2}}\|^{\frac{\frac{\Gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}{\frac{\Gamma}{2}}}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}(\Omega))}\|\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\Gamma}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}\leq c\delta^{\frac{1}{4\Gamma}}.

Similarly, we get

(5.239)

\delta\|\vartheta_{\delta}^{-2}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\leq c\delta\|\vartheta_{\delta}^{-1}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{T})}\|\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}\leq c\delta^{\frac{1}{6}}

by (
5.226
)
4,5
.

5.7.2.
Convergences as
\delta\to 0_{+}

As in previos subsections we get from (
5.225
)
2,4,5
and (
5.227
)

(5.240)

\displaystyle(\xi_{\delta},\Sigma_{\delta},\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}(\xi,\Sigma,\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle u_{\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup u

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\vartheta

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma_{\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup^{*}\sigma

\displaystyle\text{ in }(C([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega}))^{*}.

We also obtain

(5.241)

\displaystyle(\xi_{\delta},\Sigma_{\delta},\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})

\displaystyle\to(\xi,\Sigma,\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle\text{ in }C_{w}([0,T];L^{\gamma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta}

\displaystyle\to\Sigma u

\displaystyle\text{ in }C_{w}([0,T];L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta}\otimes u_{\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\Sigma u\otimes u

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\delta}|u_{\delta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\Sigma|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}),

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{\xi}_{\delta}u_{\delta},\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}u_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}u_{\delta})

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup(\mathfrak{\xi}u,\mathfrak{r}u,\mathfrak{z}u)

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+2}}(\Omega)).

5.7.3.
Pointwise convergence of
\vartheta_{\delta}
and consequences

Proceeding as in Section
5.6.5
we show that

(5.242)

\vartheta_{\delta}\to\vartheta\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}.

Indeed, by the Div–Curl lemma for

U_{\delta}=\left(\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta}),\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})u_{\delta}+\frac{\kappa_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}\right),V_{\delta}=\left(T_{k}(\vartheta_{\delta}),0,0,0\right),

where

(5.243)

\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})=\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})+\delta(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})\log\vartheta_{\delta},

it follows that

(5.244)

\overline{\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)T_{k}(\vartheta)}=\overline{\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)}\ \overline{T_{k}(\vartheta)}

as
\delta\left(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\right)\log\vartheta_{\delta}T_{k}(\vartheta_{\delta})\to 0
in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
implied by (
5.236
) yields

\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})T_{k}(\vartheta_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\overline{\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)T_{k}(\vartheta)}\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

(5.245)

\overline{b(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})G(\vartheta)}=\overline{b(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})}\ \overline{G(\vartheta)}

for any
b\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\nabla b\in C_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and Lipschtz
G\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. Fixing such an arbitrary
b
and
G
, defining
Z_{\delta}=(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})
and

b_{\delta}=b(Z_{\delta})

we obtain
b_{\delta}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
as
(Z_{\delta},u_{\delta})
satisfy the renormalized continuity equation with the renormalizing function
b
. Hence the sequence
\{t\mapsto\int_{\Omega}b_{\delta}\phi\}
is equi–continuous and bounded in
C([0,T])
for any
\phi\in C^{1}_{c}(\Omega)
. Using the Arzelà–Ascoli theorem and the density of
C^{1}_{c}(\Omega)
in
L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)

p^{\prime}\in[1,\infty)
, we get at least for a nonrelabeled subsequence

b_{\delta}\to\overline{b(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})}\text{ in }C_{w}([0,T];L^{p}(\Omega)),\ p\in(1,\overline{\gamma})

performing a diagonalization procedure. Accordingly, it follows that

b_{\delta}\to\overline{b(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})}\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,2}(\Omega))

in view of the compact embedding
L^{p}(\Omega)
into
W^{-1,2}(\Omega)
valid for
p>\frac{6}{5}
. Obviously, we get

G(\vartheta_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\overline{G(\vartheta)}\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,2}(\Omega)).

Combining the latter two convergences we conclude (
5.245
). Then we can proceed as in Section
5.6.5
to deduce

\overline{\mathfrak{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)T_{k}(\vartheta)}\geq\overline{\mathfrak{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)}\ \overline{T_{k}(\vartheta)}

for
\mathfrak{G}(r,z,\vartheta)=r\mathsf{s}_{+}(r,\vartheta)+z\mathsf{s}_{-}(z,\vartheta)
. Moreover, we have

\overline{\vartheta^{3}T_{k}(\vartheta)}\geq\overline{\vartheta^{3}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\vartheta)}

by Lemma
3.5
. Combining the last two inequalities and (
5.244
) we get
\overline{\vartheta^{3}T_{k}(\vartheta)}=\overline{\vartheta^{3}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\vartheta)}
. We conclude

(5.246)

\vartheta_{\delta}\to\vartheta\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}

using the arguments leading to (
5.188
). Accordingly, bound (
5.230
) implies

(5.247)

\vartheta_{\delta}\to\vartheta\text{ in }L^{p}(Q_{T}),\ p\in[1,p_{\beta}).

5.7.4.
Derivation of the effective viscous flux identity

As in Section
5.6.4
we obtain

(5.248)

\mathbb{S}(\vartheta_{\delta},\nabla u_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

We denote

p(r,\zeta,\vartheta)=\mathsf{P}_{+}(r,\vartheta)+\mathsf{P}_{-}(\zeta r,\vartheta)

and defining
\zeta_{\delta}=\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}/_{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}
we realize that

(5.249)

p(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\zeta_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})=p(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\zeta,\vartheta)+D_{\delta},

where

D_{\delta}=\mathsf{P}_{+}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})+\mathsf{P}_{-}(\zeta_{\delta}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})-\mathsf{P}_{+}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\vartheta)-\mathsf{P}_{-}(\zeta_{\delta}\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\vartheta).

Employing the first order Taylor expansion, the assumptions on growth from (
2.17
), (
3.5
) for
\zeta_{\delta}
,
\zeta=\mathfrak{z}/_{\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{r}
and convergence (
5.247
) we conclude

\lim_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\|D_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}=0.

Employing (
5.233
), the latter limit, convergence (
5.247
) in (
5.249
) we deduce

(5.250)

\mathcal{P}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\frac{b}{3}\vartheta^{4}+\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T})

as
\delta(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{\Gamma}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}^{\Gamma}+\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{2}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}^{2})\to 0
in
L^{1}(Q_{T})
due to (
5.232
).

As convergences (
5.241
)
1,5
and (
5.4
) are available, we can pass to the limit
\delta\to 0_{+}
in (
5.162
) with
u=u_{\delta}
and
r
standing for
\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}
or
\mathfrak{z}_{\delta}
or
\Sigma_{\delta}
or
\xi_{\delta}
to recover (
4.7
).

Using in (
5.163
) convergences (
5.241
)
2,3
, (
5.248
), (
5.250
) and
(\Sigma u)_{0,\delta}\to(\Sigma u)_{0}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
following from (
5.2
) we arrive at

(5.251)

\int_{\Omega}\Sigma u\cdot\varphi(t)-\int_{\Omega}(\Sigma u)_{0}\cdot\varphi(0)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(\Sigma u\cdot\partial_{t}\varphi+\left((\Sigma u\otimes u-\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\right)\cdot\nabla\varphi+\left(\frac{b}{3}\vartheta^{4}+\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{\zeta},\vartheta)}}\right)\operatorname{div}\varphi\right)

for all
t\in[0,T]
and any
\phi\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
.
Similarly to Section
5.6.4
we set

\varphi=\psi\phi\mathfrak{U}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})),\ \psi\in C^{1}_{c}((0,T)),\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega)

in (
5.163
) and

\varphi=\psi\phi\mathfrak{U}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})),\ \psi\in C^{1}_{c}((0,T)),\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega)

in (
5.251
) and perform the passage
\delta\to 0_{+}
to obtain

(5.252)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}}-\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)=\\
&\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\overline{\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}))}-\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(\overline{1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})})\right)\\
&+\lim_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\left((u_{\delta})_{j}\cdot(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(\phi(\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta})_{i}))-\phi(\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta})_{i}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}))-u_{j}\cdot(1_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(\phi\Sigma u))\right.\\
&\quad\quad\left.-\phi(\Sigma u)_{i}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(1_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})})\right).\end{split}

Then as
\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}
fulfills the renormalized continuity equation with the renormalizing function
b(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})=T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})
we immediately get that
T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\to\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}
in
C([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega))
for any
q\in[1,\infty)
and we conclude as in Section
5.6.4
by Lemma
3.7
that

\left(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(\phi(\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta})_{i})-\phi(\Sigma_{\delta}u_{\delta})_{i}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}))\right)(t)\rightharpoonup\left(1_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(\phi\Sigma u)-\phi(\Sigma u)_{i}\mathfrak{R}_{ij}(1_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})})\right)(t)

first in
L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\Omega)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Using the compact embedding
L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\Omega)
in
W^{-1,2}(\Omega)
for
\gamma>\frac{6}{5}
we get that the latter convergence is strong in
W^{-1,2}(\Omega)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Consequently, by the Vitali convergence theorem we get that the convergence is strong in
L^{p}(0,T;W^{-1,2}(\Omega))
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
which in combination with (
5.240
)
2
justifies that the limit on the right hand side of (
5.252
) vanishes. Hence we arrive at

(5.253)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}}-\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\ T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\overline{\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}))}-\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}))\right).\end{split}

We focus our attention on the terms on the right hand side of the latter equality. As in Section
5.6.4
we have

(5.254)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\overline{\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(1_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}))}=\lim_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta_{\delta})+\eta(\vartheta_{\delta})\right)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}\\
&+\lim_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\omega(\vartheta_{\delta},u_{\delta})\end{split}

and

(5.255)

\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\mathbb{S}(\vartheta,\nabla u)\cdot\mathfrak{R}(1_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})})=\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\phi\left(\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta)+\eta(\vartheta)\right)\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\operatorname{div}u+\int_{0}^{T}\psi\int_{\Omega}\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\omega(\vartheta,u),

where
\omega(\theta,u)
is defined in (
5.208
).

Next, according to Lemma
3.8
we infer

(5.256)

\|\omega(\vartheta_{\delta},u_{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{k,p}(\Omega))}\leq c\text{ for some }k\in(0,1),p>1

by virtue of bounds (
5.227
) and (
5.229
)
1
which also guarantee that

(5.257)

\|\omega(\vartheta_{\delta},u_{\delta})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c\text{ for some }q>1.

Following the reasoning leading to (
5.211
) we conclude
considering sequences
\{U_{\delta}\}
and
\{V_{\delta}\}
of the four dimensional vector fields

\begin{split}U_{\delta}=&\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}),T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})u_{\delta}\right),\ V_{\delta}=\left(\omega(\vartheta_{\delta},u_{\delta}),0,0,0\right)\end{split}

that

(5.258)

\omega(\vartheta_{\delta},u_{\delta})T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\omega(\vartheta,u)\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T})

since in accordance with convergences (
5.240
)
2
, (
5.247
) respectively, and estimate (
2.2
) it follows that
\overline{\omega(\vartheta,u)}=\omega(\vartheta,u)
.
Eventually, going back to (
5.253
), where we employ (
5.254
), (
5.255
) and (
5.258
), we arrive at the desired effective viscous flux identity

(5.259)

\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}}-\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}=\left(\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta)+\eta(\vartheta)\right)\left(\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\operatorname{div}u}-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\operatorname{div}u\right).

5.7.5.
Pointwise convergence of
\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}
and consequences

Firstly, we notice that by (
5.225
)
2
and (
5.232
) we have

(5.260)

\begin{split}\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}+\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{q}(Q_{T})}\leq c&\text{ for }q\in[1,\overline{\gamma}],\\
\|L_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq c&\text{ for }q\in[1,\gamma)\end{split}

for a constant
c
independent of
k,\delta
. We note that
T_{k}
is defined in (
5.130
) and

L_{k}(r)=\int_{1}^{r}\frac{T_{k}(s)}{s^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

Next, we know that
(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},u_{\delta})
fulfills the renormalized continuity equation with renormalizing function
b(r)=L_{k}(r)
and
(\mathfrak{r},u)
as well. Considering the difference of these equations with the test function being
1
we get

\int_{\Omega}\left(L_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\operatorname{div}u-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}\right)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\right)\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. We note that
\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\in L^{q}(Q_{T})
due to (
5.260
)
1
. Performing the passage
\delta\to 0_{+}
and employing effective viscous flux identity (
5.259
) we have

(5.261)

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)(t)=&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\operatorname{div}u\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta)+\eta(\vartheta)}\left(\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}}\right)\end{split}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Here
\overline{L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}
is the weak limit of
\{L_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\}
in
C_{w}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega))
, the function space is determined by (
5.260
)
2
and the fact that each
\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}
satisfies the continuity equation in the renormalized form with
b=L_{k}
. Employing Lemma
3.5
and (
2.5
) we deduce that the last integral on the right hand side of (
5.261
) is nonpositive implying

\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-L_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)(t)\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\operatorname{div}u

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Performing the passage
k\to\infty
in the latter inequality we conclude

\overline{\mathfrak{r}\log\mathfrak{r}}=\mathfrak{r}\log\mathfrak{r}\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}

provided we show that

(5.262)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\operatorname{div}u=0.

For
\gamma<\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}
we get by virtue of (
5.232
), (
5.260
) and the interpolation that

(5.263)

\begin{split}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\operatorname{div}u\right|&\leq\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}\|\operatorname{div}u\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}\\
&\leq c\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|^{\lambda}_{L^{\overline{\gamma}}(Q_{T})}\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|^{1-\lambda}_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\\
&\leq c\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|^{1-\lambda}_{L^{1}(Q_{T})},\end{split}

where
\lambda=\frac{\overline{\gamma}}{2(\overline{\gamma}-1)}
. Furthermore, we have using the weak lower semicontinuity of
L^{1}
–norm

\begin{split}\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}&\leq\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-\mathfrak{r}\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}+\|\mathfrak{r}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\\
&\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}+\|\mathfrak{r}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\leq ck^{-q}\end{split}

for some
q>0
. Performing the passage
k\to\infty
we get
\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{1}(Q_{T})}\to 0
. Hence going back to (
5.263
) we conclude (
5.262
). If
\gamma=\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}
then
\overline{\gamma}=2
and the interpolation above cannot be applied. Instead, we show that so called oscillation defect measure is bounded, i.e.,

(5.264)

\sup_{k\geq 1}\limsup_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}+1}(Q_{T})}<\infty.

Then as
\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}+1>2
we use
\|\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}+1}(Q_{T})}
for the interpolation in (
5.263
) and employ the fact that the latter norm is estimated by the oscillation defect measure, which as we already know is bounded. Let us show (
5.264
). Employing the decomposition from (
2.11
) in (
5.259
) we obtain

\begin{split}&\underline{\pi}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-\overline{\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}}-\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta)+\eta(\vartheta)\right)\left(\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\operatorname{div}u}-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\operatorname{div}u+\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\operatorname{div}u\right).\end{split}

As
r\mapsto p(r,\zeta,\vartheta)
is nondecreasing we get using Lemma
3.5
that the second integral on the left hand side of the latter inequality is nonnegative. Moreover, due to the convexity of
r\mapsto r^{\gamma}
, we conclude employing (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) that

\begin{split}&\left(\overline{\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}}-\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}\right)\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-\overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)+\limsup_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}-\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}\right)\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}-\overline{\mathfrak{r}^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}}}\ \overline{T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})}\right)\\
&\leq c\limsup_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(1+\vartheta)^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{3}\mu(\vartheta)+\eta(\vartheta)\right)\left(\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\right)\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}+\left(T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})\right)\operatorname{div}u\right)(1+\vartheta)\\
&\leq c\limsup_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}+1}(Q_{T})}\left(\|\operatorname{div}u_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}+\|\operatorname{div}u\|_{L^{2}(Q_{T})}\right)\|1+\vartheta\|_{L^{p_{\beta}}(Q_{T})}.\end{split}

The first term on the first line of the latter inequality is nonnegative since
s\mapsto s^{\gamma}
is convex and
s\mapsto T_{k}(s)
is concave. Moreover, as
|T_{k}(s_{1})-T_{k}(s_{2})|\leq|s_{1}-s_{2}|
,
|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\leq|s_{1}^{\gamma}-s_{2}^{\gamma}|
,
\gamma>1
we have

\limsup_{\delta\to 0_{+}}\|T_{k}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\mathfrak{r})\|_{L^{\overline{\gamma}_{\beta}+1}(Q_{T})}\leq c,

where
c
is independent of
k
, due to (
5.227
) and (
5.230
). Hence (
5.264
) immediately follows.
Again, using the strict convexity of
s\mapsto s\log s
and (
3.5
) we conclude

(5.265)

\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}\to\mathfrak{r},\ \mathfrak{z}_{\delta}\to\mathfrak{z}\text{ a.e. in }Q_{T}

implying

(5.266)

(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})\to(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z})\text{ in }L^{p}(Q_{T})\text{ for any }p\in[1,\overline{\gamma}).

Obviously, convergences (
5.242
), (
5.265
) yield
\overline{\overline{p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)}}=p(\mathfrak{r},\zeta,\vartheta)
in (
5.251
), i.e., we have shown that (
4.8
) is satisfied.

5.7.6.
Limit passage
\delta\to 0_{+}
in the energy equality

Having convergences (
5.242
), (
5.265
) and bound (
5.234
) at hand we get

\mathcal{E}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\to\mathcal{E}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

Hence employing (
5.241
)
4
, the latter convergence, (
5.5
)
1,3,6
, bounds (
5.225
)
3
and (
5.226
)
4
in (
5.166
) we conclude (
4.9
).

5.7.7.
Limit passage
\delta\to 0_{+}
in the entropy balance

By virtue of (
5.242
) and (
5.265
) we deduce

(5.267)

\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\to\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))\text{ for some }q>\frac{6}{5}

using the Vitali convergence theorem due to (
5.235
). Recalling that
\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})=\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})+\delta(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta}+\mathfrak{z}_{\delta})\log\vartheta_{\delta}
we have by virtue of (
5.236
) and (
5.267
)

(5.268)

\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})\to\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))\text{ for some }q>\frac{6}{5}.

Moreover, taking into consideration the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,2}(\Omega)
to
L^{6}(\Omega)
and (
5.240
)
2
we infer

(5.269)

\mathcal{S}_{\delta}(\mathfrak{r}_{\delta},\mathfrak{z}_{\delta},\vartheta_{\delta})u_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)u\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

Next, using convergence (
5.242
) and bound (
5.230
) it follows that

(5.270)

\vartheta_{\delta}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\to\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\text{ in }L^{p}(Q_{T})\text{ for some }p>2.

Hence we have due to (
5.229
)
1

\nabla\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\nabla\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\text{ in }L^{2}(Q_{T})

and by virtue of (
2.4
)

\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})\vartheta^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}_{\delta}\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\geq 1\}}\to\kappa(\vartheta)\vartheta^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\chi_{\{\vartheta\geq 1\}}\text{ in }L^{2}(Q_{T}).

Combining the latter two convergences we get that

(5.271)

\frac{\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\geq 1\}}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}=\frac{2}{\beta}\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})\vartheta_{\delta}^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\geq 1\}}\nabla(\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\frac{2}{\beta}\kappa(\vartheta)\vartheta^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\chi_{\{\vartheta\geq 1\}}\nabla(\vartheta^{\frac{\beta}{2}})=\frac{\kappa(\vartheta)}{\vartheta}\chi_{\{\vartheta\geq 1\}}\nabla\vartheta\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

Moreover, we have

(5.272)

\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\leq 1\}}\to\kappa(\vartheta)\chi_{\{\vartheta\leq 1\}}\text{ in }L^{p}(Q_{T})\text{ for any }p\in[1,\infty)

by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and (
5.242
). Next, as a consequence of (
5.228
), (
5.242
) and the Vitali convergence theorem we obtain

\log\vartheta_{\delta}\to\log\vartheta\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T})

and accordingly

\nabla\log\vartheta_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\nabla\log\vartheta\text{ in }L^{2}(Q_{T}).

Combining the latter convergence and (
5.272
) we get

(5.273)

\frac{\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})}{\vartheta_{\delta}}\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\leq 1\}}\nabla\vartheta_{\delta}=\kappa(\vartheta_{\delta})\chi_{\{\vartheta_{\delta}\leq 1\}}\nabla\log\vartheta_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\kappa(\vartheta)\chi_{\{\vartheta\leq 1\}}\nabla\log\vartheta=\frac{\kappa(\vartheta)}{\vartheta}\chi_{\{\vartheta\leq 1\}}\nabla\vartheta\text{ in }L^{1}(Q_{T}).

The next task is the recovery of (
4.11
). To this end we employ convergences (
5.5
)
4,5
, (
5.240
)
4
, (
5.268
), (
5.269
), (
5.271
), (
5.273
) and bounds (
5.226
)
4,5
, (
5.239
) respectively to pass to the limit
\delta\to 0_{+}
in (
5.165
). We note that inequality (
4.10
) holds. Indeed, going back to (
5.164
) we observe that the
\delta
–dependent quantities are nonnegative and we already know that for the limit passage in the remaining terms the weak lower semicontinuity of convex functionals is sufficient, cf. Section
5.5.3
.

6.
Proof of Theorem
4.4

We set

F(\alpha)=\frac{1}{f_{+}(\alpha)},\ G(\alpha)=\frac{1}{f_{-}(1-\alpha)}

and obtain that
F
and
G
are strictly monotone non-vanishing functions on
[0,1]
by assumptions on
f_{\pm}
. We denote

\begin{split}\underline{F}&=\min\{F(\underline{\alpha}),F(\overline{\alpha})\},\overline{F}=\max\{F(\underline{\alpha}),F(\overline{\alpha})\},\\
\underline{G}&=\min\{G(\underline{\alpha}),G(\overline{\alpha})\},\overline{G}=\max\{G(\underline{\alpha}),G(\overline{\alpha})\}.\end{split}

Obviously,
F:[\underline{\alpha},\overline{\alpha}]\to[\underline{F},\overline{F}]
and
G:[\underline{\alpha},\overline{\alpha}]\to[\underline{G},\overline{G}]
are
C^{1}
–diffeomorphisms. We intend to employ Theorem
4.3
with initial conditions

\begin{split}\xi_{0}=\rho_{0},\ \mathfrak{r}_{0}=f_{+}(\alpha_{0})\rho_{0},\ \mathfrak{z}_{0}=f_{-}(1-\alpha_{0})z_{0},\ \Sigma_{0}=\rho_{0}+z_{0},\ u_{0},\\
\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\vartheta)(0,\cdot)=\frac{4b}{3}\vartheta_{0}^{3}+\rho_{0}s_{+}\left(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\alpha_{0}},\vartheta_{0}\right)+z_{0}s_{-}\left(\frac{z_{0}}{1-\alpha_{0}},\vartheta_{0}\right).\end{split}

If
(\alpha_{0},\rho_{+,0},\rho_{-,0})
fulfill conditions in
(
4.12
)
1
then
(\xi_{0},\mathfrak{r}_{0}.\mathfrak{z}_{0},\Sigma_{0})
fulfill condition

(6.1)

\underline{a}\mathfrak{r}_{0}\leq\mathfrak{z}_{0}\leq\overline{a}\mathfrak{r}_{0},\ \min\{\underline{F},\underline{G}\}\Sigma_{0}\leq\mathfrak{r}_{0}+\mathfrak{z}_{0}\leq\max\{\overline{F},\overline{G}\}\Sigma_{0},\ \underline{F}\mathfrak{r}_{0}\leq\xi\leq\overline{F}\mathfrak{r}_{0}.

Hence Theorem
4.3
guarantees the global in time existence of a bounded energy weak solution
(\xi,\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{z},\Sigma,u,\vartheta)
to (
1.22
) in the sense of Definition
4
.

We define in accordance with convention (
2.19
)
\alpha=F^{-1}\left(\xi/_{a}\mathfrak{r}\right)
,
\rho=F(\alpha)\mathfrak{r}
and
z=G(\alpha)\mathfrak{z}
. Obviously, by virtue of Lemma
3.10

(1)
the quantities
\xi/_{a}\mathfrak{r}
and
\alpha
fulfill transport equation (
4.2
) with initial conditions
\xi/_{a}\mathfrak{r}_{0}=F(\alpha_{0})
,
\alpha(0)=\alpha_{0}
respectively and
\alpha
possesses the regularity specified in Definition
4
. Applying Lemma
3.10

(2)
we infer that the quantities
\rho
and
z
possess the regularity specified in Definition
4
. The pairs
(\rho,u)
and
(z,u)
satisfy continuity equation (
4.1
) with initial condition
\rho(0)=F(\alpha_{0})\mathfrak{r}_{0}
,
z(0)=G(\alpha_{0})\mathfrak{z}_{0}
respectively. We now show that

(6.2)

\Sigma(t,x)=(\rho+z)(t,x)\text{ for all }t\in[0,T]\text{ and a.a. }x\in\Omega.

Firstly, we observe that
z/_{a}\rho
satisfies the transport equation with the initial condition
\frac{z_{0}}{\rho_{0}}
. The pair
(T,u)
, where
T=\Sigma/_{a}\mathfrak{r}
fulfills a transport equation with the initial datum

(6.3)

T(0)=F(\alpha_{0})+F(\alpha_{0})\frac{z_{0}}{\rho_{0}}

according to Lemma
3.10

(1)
. We have that
(S,u)
, where
S=\rho/_{a}\mathfrak{r}+z/_{a}\mathfrak{r}
, satisfies the transport equation with the initial condition equal to the expression on the right hand side of (
6.3
). Hence it follows that
\mathfrak{r}T(t,x)=\mathfrak{r}S(t,x)
for all
t\in[0,T]
and a.a.
x\in\Omega
by Lemma
3.11
, which concludes (
6.2
). Accordingly, we observe that momentum equation (
4.3
) is fulfilled. Taking into account the definition of the energies
\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}
and entropies
\mathfrak{s}_{\pm}
in (
1.20
) we conclude from (
4.9
) and (
4.11
) that energy equality (
4.4
) and entropy balance (
4.6
) are satisfied.
